SF69 A surface shaft located on the second alleyway north of Bellister's House.
SF70 A locked surface shaft within the cellar of Piergeiron's Palace. It was guarded at all times by five City Guard and an armar. Whenever they saw or heard anything suspicious from the sewers, they would sound alarm on the wall.
SF72 The lair of Xanathar, located directly beneath the Philosopher's Court.
SF73 North entrance into the Citadel of the Bloody Hand. Connected the secondary passage west of SF53 with the once-secret northern entrance. There was a guard station here, once used to defend Castle Waterdeep from subterranean attacks.
SF74 Southern entrance into the Citadel of the Bloody Hand. Connected the secondary passages running north and west of SF48 with the once-secret southern entrance.
SF75 A collapsed section of sewer floor and flooded storage vault of a forgotten merchant, used as a lair by the Savants of the Dark Tide.

Entrances into the sewers that were not labeled on the map included the following locations:
Dragon Tower A wizard's tower in the Sea Ward that had a secret entrance to the sewers hidden in its first floor kitchen.
God Catcher Sometime after 1385 DR this sunken, former Walking Statue in the Castle Ward had its leg hollowed out to create an access point to the sewers (see "History" for more information).
Golden Hammer An inn with a trapdoor that opened into the sewers.
Jalanvaloss This steel dragon had secret apartments in the House of Pride and Meiroth's Fine Silks that had entry stairs linking to the cellars, which linked to the sewers. They also had a secret cache somewhere within the city.
Lightning Targe A newer adventuring club that had a rather mildewy tunnel connection to the sewers.
Safehaven An inn in the Southern Ward, whose cellar contained a hidden ladder leading down to the sewers. This ladder was hidden behind a  tall door, which opened upon turning upward the spigot on the last wine casket.
Thirsty Sailor A tavern in the Dock Ward that had some kind of entrance into the Smugglers' Run section of the sewers in its back alley.

Groups of Interest

Cellarers' & Plumbers' Guild Most members of this guild learned the general layout of the sewers after two to five years. This is because their primary concerns were keeping the sewers of the city functional and safe, with guild members being engaged in constant small repair jobs in the sewers, such as on plumbing in old buildings. They also possessed maps of the sewers, but they never sold them.
City Guard In the late-14 century DR, it became the duty of the City Guard to police the sewers.
City Watch Up until the late-14 century DR, it was the duty of the City Watch to police the sewers. After this authority switched over to the City Guard, watchmen rarely ever entered the sewers. Only doing so if pursuing a suspect or when they discovered sewer tunnels that connected with cellars. All Watch captains possessed keys to the sewer access points of their assigned ward.
Confluence The lower status members of this secret society used secret passages within the sewers to enter Tespergates, their organization's base of operations, by means of the villa's cellars.
Cult of the Rat A beast cult that worshiped Sneel, the lesser deity of rats, lived and operated in secrecy in the sewers throughout the 14 and 15 centuries. Many of its members were individuals who allowed themselves to be afflicted with the wererat form of lycanthropy. They often had offerings to Sneel in their warrens, meant to try and earn his favor.
Dungsweepers' Guild This guild normally did not work on the sewer system, but for security reasons the city reserved the right to contact the guildmaster and hire members to help in such work.
Guild of Watermen Members of this guild knew various spots where the sewer system emptied into the harbor.
Merfolk Living within Deepwater Harbor, these people regularly patrolled the areas where the sewers emptied. They would use pole mounted catch-nets to scoop up and gather any debris they found into large tow-globes, which they then used to transport the debris far out to sea.
Plague Rats This elite organization of thieves and assassins operated mainly under the cover of darkness in this sewer network and the Dock Ward.
Savants of the Dark Tide This aboleth-led criminal organization had its base of operations within this sewer network and mainly operated within it.
Unseen This secret society operated out of both the sewers and Rat Hills for decades, but following the Rat Hills Conflagration they began having their main meetings within secret rooms in the sewers. Their leader, Hlaavin, also kept many safe houses within the sewers.
Xanathar's Thieves' Guild This guild had safehouses scattered throughout the sewers, hidden by secret doors. Tunnels were often marked with the symbol of the guild in yellow chalk, helping to guide members to the safehouses. Every few days these were erased, either by members of the guild or by the Dungsweepers' Guild. The guild also utilized some of the stone dwarven portals that were scattered around the sewers.

History
These sewers were built over the course of many generations, dating as far back as 1162 DR. As far back as this date, there existed three levels to the sewer system.

During the days of Ahghairon, a number of Underdark creatures tried to invade the sewers and Waterdeep proper by means of a portal within Undermountain, which he put an end to by means of the gatewarp spell.

At some point a group of dwarves established a kingdom beneath the sewers, which eventually fell into ruin and became a mere legend on the surface. Their ruins were later discovered by the archeaologist Wently Kelso.

In 1208 DR, in the month of Kythorn, some unauthorized construction was discovered on the second level of the sewers.

After securing a stronghold in 1262 DR (that would later be renamed the Citadel of the Bloody Handy), the Shadow Thieves constructed for it two links to the sewers of Waterdeep.

In 1280 DR, in the month of Mirtul, some trapped sewer gas was cleared from an area on the third sewer level. A year later, on the month of Kythorn, a cistern area was added to the second level of the sewers. And in 1292 DR, on the month of Tarsakh, flood control gates on the second level were repaired and additional ones were installed.

In 1298 DR, the Shadow Thieves were driven out of the city and the Lords of Waterdeep had the citadel's two passages to the sewers garrisoned by the City Guard. From then on they were occasionally employed by high-ranking occupants of Castle Waterdeep when a discreet exit was desired.

Some time in the late 1350's DR, on Mirtul 15, twelve tanystropheus eggs ordered by Phalantar's Philtres & Components hatched and escaped into the sewers via a garbage chute. That night a crew of Cellarers were attacked by the young dinosaurs while laying a large, heavy length of pipe. One member was killed, while two others were badly mauled. Frustrated by this, the guild offered a reward of 200 gold pieces for their capture (dead or alive), while Phalantar Orivan himself offered 500 gp for live specimens and 100 gp for dead ones. By the end of Uktar, it was assumed that any tanystropheuses not recovered were dead from the winter weather.

Around this decade, false and terribly inaccurate maps of the sewers were being sold by people trying to pass themselves off as Cellarers.

In 1355 DR, in the months of Ches and Tarsakh, flood control blocks were proposed for some areas of the upper sewer level.

In 1357 DR, a man from Luskan by the name of Neruudan set about making a smuggling ring in Waterdeep. To that end, he hired an evil mage to clear out the upper sewers. The mage put a giant crocodile in the sewers, with his quasit familiar riding upon its back and directing the beast, killing members of the Cellarers and Plumbers Guild until they began to refuse to do work. However, Neruudan was unaware of the sewers' use as a highway by locals and their wide selection of dangerous fauna, which eventually lead to the downfall of his plans.

In 1358 DR, during the Time of Troubles, the mortal avatar of Myrkul opened a portal between Hades and the Prime Material plane. Hoards of undead came rushing out through the sewers and up into the Dock Ward. The guardsman Ylarell tracked these undead and in the process was slain by Myrkul. Five Cellarers also became lost in the sewers during this time, having been transformed into cursts that continued to patrol the sewers for decades.

In the wake of Myrkul's destruction a deathshrieker was created in the sewers beneath what later became known as Cynosure. It would stalk the sewers for over a decade.

Also during this year, on Marpenoth 7 an adventuring band received orders from the Lords of Waterdeep to travel into the city's sewers to put an end to the ongoing plans of Xanathar. Before embarking into their depths the adventuring band was gifted by Khelben Arunsun with a partial map of the sewers and a copy of Wently Kelso's The World Beneath Waterdeep, which included several notes on the hazards and creatures they could expect to encounter. Secretly aided by another beholder, the adventurers managed to slay the crime lord and present his eye stalk to the Lords of Waterdeep, while the other beholder assumed the identity of Xanathar and took over his criminal organization.

In Eleint, 1367 DR, new creatures began to be encountered in the sewers by members of the Plumbers' Guild, such as sewerms. Some of these new creatures originated from the Rat Hills, such as gulguthydrae, having fled into the sewers following the Rat Hills Conflagration.

In 1369 DR, during the event known as Halaster's Higharvestide, a number of creatures crawled out from the sewers and terrorized the people of Waterdeep. Following this event, the guard station located near the northern entrance to the Citadel of the Bloody Hand became primarily concerned with guarding against the living spells that still remained after Halaster's Higharvestide.

That same year the city faced an attack by the forces of Iakhovas. In the wake of that repelled invasion a number of sea creatures escaped into the sewer system, with some of them, like the malenti Laaqueel, having been led into the sewers through a surface shaft at Coin Alley by Iakhovas himself.

Among the dozens of aboleths that fought in the army of Iakhovas there was one trio, hailing from the Swordsea Depths, who had joined in this assault for the sole purpose of using the chaos to sneak their way into the city's sewer system. Within the sewers the would-be Savants of the Dark Tide established a lair, then over time gradually enslaved members of the Cellarers’ & Plumbers’ Guild and transformed rogues into skum servitors.

Around 1372 DR, the doomsphere Xliiqil began stalking the sewers, trying to learn what happened to the Company of Crazed Venturers who had slayed it decades prior. Also around this time, eyeballs and lurking stranglers roamed the sewers on behalf of Xanathar. After the event known as the Silence of Lolth began in Eleasis 28, some drow fled into the depths of the Lowerdark and displaced the rylkar, a rat-like species to known to only a few Underdark experts. Moving to higher subterannean depths, the rylkar began infesting sewers in Faerûn, including the Sewers of Waterdeep.

In 1385 DR, the Spellplague hit Toril and caused all of the Walking Statues of Waterdeep to become animated and wreak havoc across the city. The Blackstaff at the time, Tsarra Chaadren, turned the ground to mud underneath a statue known as the God Catcher. It sank partway into the ground, one of its legs penetrating a sewer line. Once the statues became immobile the God Catcher remained where it had sunken and was eventually converted into a tenement building, while its leg was hollowed out to create an access point to the sewer system.

Around In the late 15 century DR, a number of dwarves attempting to dig larger homes for themselves in Undercliff inadvertently breach Waterdeep's sewers in the process.

In, shortly after Mistshore was largely destroyed in a massive fire, Xanathar had some areas of the sewer network diverted in order to give his organization more space, which resulted in a large daily influx of sewage into Deepwater Harbor. Merfolk who lived nearby made attempts to clean the area, but were slaughtered by an undead eye of the deep under Xanathar's command. Eventually, Laeral Silverhand intervened — the meddlings of Xanathar were undone, the eye of the deep was slain, and harbor waters were revitalized.

In 1492 DR, Jeryth Phaulkon sent awakened rats into the sewers to search for Xanathar's hidden lair, eventually discovering a secret spiral stairwell in the sewers beneath the Castle Ward.

Rumors & Legends
 Some rumors claimed that the cellars of some of the City Watch's guardposts contained secret entrances to the sewers.
 Some claimed that members of the Cellarers' & Plumbers' Guild could purposely cause the sewers to back up and overflow into certain buildings as an intimidation tactic.
 The Cliffwatch Inn's cellars were rumored to have secret passages to the sewers.
 There were persistent rumors that the notorious thief Uluryk Malogh made an extensive map of the city's cellars and sewers, detailing all their various barriers, locks, and traps.
 Around the mid-14 century DR, there were rumors circulating that Xanathar's Thieves' Guild had trained guard rats roaming the sewers and that slavers dwelling within the uppermost rooms of Undermountain creeped out into the sewers at night to kidnap unsuspecting Waterdhavians.

## Watchman's helm

Watchman's helms were enchanted helmets that received their names from Helm, the god of guardians, or for simply being popular among watchmen.

Powers
These helmets magically enchanted the wearer's senses of sight, and hearing, making them invaluable for guards and members fo the watch. All versions of Watchman's helms radiated dim light, sometimes of blue hue, at the distance of .

History
By the late 14 century DR, these helmets were in high demand among folk who made living by guarding people, possessions, and places. However, Watchman's helms were just as popular among thieves and rogues for the same reasons.

In 1374 DR, a Watchman's helm was offered to brave adventurers who were strong enough to exterminate undead creatures out of the Mere of Dead Men.

Notable Owners
Irim Mendar, a purveyor of exotic and enchanted goods from Samargol circa 1374 DR.
Maegel: a dead adventurer whose body was haunted by his ghost deep inside a cave south of Port Llast in 1372 DR.
Neurik: a cleric of Tyr in Port Llast circa 1372 DR.

## Wand of summoning

Wands of summoning were magic wands that allowed for a temporary summoning of creatures under the wielder's control.

Powers
These wands allowed the user to cast the summon creature IV spell, materializing several creatures under the caster's control. The effects of the wand's spell were temporary. A typical wand of summoning possessed five charges.

History
Historically, the first versions of these wands were first created by worshipers of long-forgotten beast totems. Even though these wands' origins were in divine magic, the 14 century versions were created to wield arcane magics, and even the incantations used for creation differed greatly.

## Wand of Electra

The wand of Electra was a powerful wand, similar to a wand of lightning bolts.

Description
The wand of Electra was an unassuming iron rod.

Powers
This rod allowed the wearer to cast the lightning bolt spell at will. It possessed four charges.

History
In 1369 DR, the ring of Electra and its sister item, the wand of Electra, could be found in the Cult of the Dragon-controlled upper levels of Eleven Catacombs underneath Castle Cormanthor. Each of the enchanted items was placed in a chest on the opposite rooms of a small healing alcove. Each room was guarded by several skeleton lords who came to life and attacked anyone who was bold enough to steal elven treasures.

## Wand of negative energy

Wands of negative energy were offensive magic wands imbued with arcane negative energy.

Description
These wands were crafted out of elven femurs. The bone shafts were smoothed and yellowed by many hands that held these wands since their creation. The gruesomeness of these magic items was accentuated by fetishes that dangled off these wands. The negative energy at the core of these wands made anyone touching them feel the unpleasant sensation of their flesh crawling.

Powers
Usable by bards, sorcerers, and wizards, fully-charged wands of negative energy held fifty uses of the negative energy ray.

History
In the late 14 century DR, these evil-looking wands could be found throughout the Sword Coast region, notably in the cities of Neverwinter and Daggerford.

## Water elemental myrmidon

Water elemental myrmidons, otherwise known as water myrmidons or water archons, were a variety of elemental myrmidon, beings created by conjuring and bonding elementals—in this case, of elemental water—into ritually created suits of armor to act as servile weapons of war.

Description
Water myrmidons were beings composed entirely of elemental water, with bodies that were roughly human size and shape.

Like all elemental myrmidons, they wore thick armor, typically of a plate or scale variety. These not only protected them against attacks, but contained their form and was integral to their being. After some time they were capable of removing this armor and replacing it with something else, but many never saw any need to do so. Their armor was all that would remain of them when killed.

Personality
Though they were capable of moving on land and would do so on ocassion as a surprise tactic, they preferred to remain near water. Being kept too far from water could affect their loyalty to a master. They also valued having frequent opportunities to spread misery and woe.

Abilities
Water myrmidons were immune to the effects of diseases and poisons, as they were composed wholly of elemental essence. Also, like other elemental beings, they had no need for sleep.

They were impervious to harm from water-based spells or abilities, but those that were cold-based would hinder their movements. And they were uniquely able to temporarily infuse their weapon strikes with elemental ice.

Shoal Reavers These water myrmidons could make temporary harpoons out of solid water and conjure forth small whirlpools.
Waveshapers These water myrmidons could manipulate the water by gesturing with their fans. Conjuring forth small waves, whirlpools, or geysers. As well as empowering aquatic or water elemental creatures.

Combat
The standard variety of water myrmidon, shoal reavers, typically fought with tridents. While those known as tide striders and waveshapers typically fought with greatspears and dual war fans respectively. Regardless of what weapon a water myrmidon used, it was considered magical.

Shoal reavers and tide striders both fought in close quarters, using their agility in water to their advantage, while watershapers fought from afar with their abilities. Shoal reavers would often use their water harpoons to pull creatures closer to the water before conjuring forth whirlpools.

History

During the Elemental Evil crisis, water myrmidons and their variants were utilized by members of one of the four elemental cults responsible for the crisis, the Cult of the Crushing Wave.

Varieties

Shoal Reavers The term used for the basic variety of water myrmidon.
Tide Striders One of the more powerful varieties of water myrmidons. They were the most agile at moving through water and known for being quite reserved and violent.
Waveshapers One of the more powerful varieties of water myrmidons. They were equivalent to war priests, aspiring for a day when Prime Material worlds would be drowned by the churning waters of the Elemental Chaos. They typically wore robes atop their armor and were known for creating great ships made of semisolid water, which could rise and submerged as they willed.

Society
Like any elemental myrmidons, when left to their own devices these beings would form societies that had the same structure, one that was roughly militaristic. They were rigidly disciplined and had hierarchies based on rank, with rank determining every aspect of life in their stronghold. These ranks did not have proper titles, as water myrmidons simply knew where they and others ranked. They did not challenge authority or compete against one another for rank, each being satisfied with their station and feeling they best serve in that position.

Free water myrmidons traveled on the great water ships crafted by waveshapers, engaging in pirate activity. They were known to construct outposts in lakes or swamps, which they traveled between by treating rivers and streams as highways.

Outside of combat, water myrmidons spent much of their time trying to hone their knowledge of strategy and tactics, engaging in tactical exercises, or sparring. Constantly training for the next conflict, which satisfied their innate desire to make war.

Creation
Some said that constructing an elemental archon required a special foundry, with a special forge built in a significant concentration of elemental energy, and a summoning crucible that served as the transformation space. These forges rarely appeared like a common forge, being unrecognizable contraptions of arcane and elemental magic.

A working foundry could produce them as often as the ritual could be performed and for as long as the foundry's resources were maintained. The forge's power determine what kind of myrmidon was created. A forge with ebbing power could only generate the most basic of water myrmidons, while those at high flow could produce more powerful varieties, such as tide striders and waveshapers.

Relationships
Many were known to serve Olhydra, the evil archomental of elemental water. Beyond this, they often found themselves in the service of masters who were like the ocean quite destructive and unpredictable in nature, such as aboleths or human pirates.

## Wazu's Wand

Wazu's Wand was a huge "wand" that possessed mysteries magical properties.

Description
This "wand" was effectively an oversized chunk of yellow crystal, shaped like an icicle with both ends sharp. It was unknown if it was wielded as a wand by some mysterious giant creature or simply the name given to the item by Torleth Mindulspeer. Wazu's Wand was as thick as an adult human and as tall as two.

History
In 1372 DR, Wazu's Wand could be found in Torleth's Treasures, a curio vendor of Gillian's Hill. The crystal regularly pulsed and rippled with arcane energies. Torleth had a white tag attached to this unique item, asking for 500 gold pieces for it.

## Wall of shadow

Wall of shadow was a divine conjuration spell used by priests devoted to Eshowdow.

Effects
This spell created a permanent wall or ring of twilight the size of which depended on the skill of the caster. The "wall" version of this spell was always oriented vertically. A wall of shadow acted like an impenetrable wall of force to anything connected to the Negative Energy plane or the Shadowfell. Any creature not connected to the Shadowfell or Negative Energy plane that passed through the wall of shadow would experience the effects of an enervation spell that lasted for several hours.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component the priest's holy symbol and an area of shadow to cast.

## Water sprint

Water sprint was a divine alteration spell used only by specialty priests devoted to Sebek called Swamplords.

Effects
This spell allowed the caster to swim three times faster than normal for one minute. The caster had several hours to use the ability before it expired once the spell was cast.

Swamplords often employed this spell while hunting, waiting in crocodile form and bursting forth with great speed to catch prey.

Components
This spell required only verbal and somatic components to cast.

## Water drake

Water drakes were a species of elemental drake.

Description
Water drakes, like other elemental drakes, were draconic in form and about  long. They had silvery-blue scales that were more fish-like than reptilian.

Personality
Water drakes were largely indifferent to other creatures.

Abilities
Water drakes were better at swimming than flying, though they were capable of surviving on land indefinitely. Their affinity for elemental water meant that their very touch doused even magical fires.

Combat
Water drakes preferred to fight below the waves, only flying to snatch prey out of the air.

Ecology
Water drakes lived along coastlands or island chains, preying on any creatures foolish enough to trespass in their territory. Since they weren't as fast as other aquatic creatures, they stuck to shallow areas. Consequently, water drakes and dragon turtles often shared similar territories, and although a lone water drake was no match for the larger dragon turtle, groups of drakes were fearsome enough to take them on. Water drakes also hated bronze dragons, but feared them even more.

In the land of Thay, they could be found in the River Lapendrar.
Languages
Water drakes were known to speak Aquan and Draconic in oddly pleasant burbling voices.

Wands of cure serious wounds were magic wands that allowed their owners to dispense powerful curative magics, on par with the most experienced clerics.

Description

These wands allowed the wielder to cast extremely potent version of the cure serious wounds. Each wand possessed up to ten charges.

Varlae: a human cleric of Bane, active circa 1372 DR.

## Wand of size alteration

A wand of size alteration was an enchanted wand allowing the wielder to change the size of other creatures.

Powers
This wand allowed the wielder to influence the size of a target creature similar to either the enlarge or reduce spell, affecting a 50% change in the target's size and all of their carried equipment. The relative strength of the creature would change accordingly. The target needed to be within  for the magic to function.

The effects of a wand of size alteration could be cancelled by a successful dispel magic spell, but doing so ran the risk of traumatically shocking the target. These wands could be recharged by wizards and each use of the wand used a single charge.

Notable Owners
Marwan al-Jabal al-Hiyali, the Grandfather of the Everlasting in Zakhara, carried a wand of size alteration.

## Wand of conjuration

A wand of conjuration was an enchanted wand that allowed the wielder to recognize any conjuration spell.

Powers
By simply grasping this wand the wielder was able to recognize any written or seen conjuration spell. The wielder could expend one of the wand's charges to cast unseen servant or monster summoning. The wand could also be used to conjure a curtain of pure blackness that absorbed all light. The curtain could attain a maximum size of  but needed to reach from floor to ceiling and wall to wall wherever it was placed. A prismatic sphere could also be created with the wand, with a single color of the sphere appearing at a time from red to violet.

Notable Owners
Adnan al-Raqi, a notorious sha'ir from Huzuz, carried a wand of conjuration.

A wand of illusion was a magic item that enabled the wielder to create visible and audible illusions.

This wand emitted an invisible ray that replicated the effects of the audible glamer and phantasmal force spells. The wielder needed to concentrate on the illusion to maintain it. A single charge was required for each component of the created illusion. Wands of illusion could be recharged.

## Wand of negation

A wand of negation was a magic item that cancelled the effects of spells and other magic items.

Powers
These wands negated the powers of certain spells and magic items. A pale beam of light would shoot from the wand when pointed at the desired target. There was a 75% chance that the targeted item would fail when used. Each use of a wand of negation expended a single charge. These wands could not be recharged.

 Chantos Greybeard owned a wand of negation.

## Wand of flame extinguishing

A wand of flame extinguishing was a magic item used to put out magical and nonmagical fires.

Powers
This item could extinguish smaller nonmagical fires without using a charge. One charge was expended to extinguish a larger normal fire or a smaller magical fire, and two charges were needed for larger magical fires. Additionally, these wands could be used to cause a great deal of harm to fire-based creatures such as fire elementals.

 Kadorr, a Zhentarim wizard, carried a wand of flame extinguishing.
 Mistletan, a half-elf druid who kept a solitary watch over the Border Forest.
 Volodar Celdomen, a businessman in Ravens Bluff, kept a wand of flame extinguishing in a secret compartment of his bedroom chest.

## Warehouse (Kulzar's Alley)

This unnamed warehouse was a warehouse located on Kulzar's Alley in Waterdeep.

Location
This building was adjoined to the buildings known as the Gildenfires and Roarke House, being situated right between them.

History
In the late 15 century DR, this warehouse and the buildings adjoining it were all owned by noble family Neverember. Around this time it was leased longterm to the noble families Ammakyl and Gralleth.

In Eleint of 1479 DR, an inspection of the warehouse found that half of it was filled with older furniture and decorations from three separate instances in which Jadzia Ammakyl decided that her mansion wasn't up to the latest Waterdhavians fashions and needed updating.

That autumn inspection also found that the other half of the warehouse was filled by the Gralleths with the belongings of noble villas the family had obtained after absorbing the interests and estates of the noble families Bladesemmer and Markarl.

Watching shadow was a divine alteration spell used by priests devoted to Mask, enabling them to see through a shadow.

This spell created a mobile shadow that allowed the caster to see anything near the shadow. The caster could move the watching shadow around by silent will, visualizing the shadow's surroundings out to a point that their own eyes would normally be able to see. Walls with lead or gorgon's blood served as an effective barrier to this spell. This spell would end if the caster began casting another spell.

## War of Unmasking

The War of Unmasking was a conflict between the Thindolese and yuan-ti of House Sseradess on the Chultan Peninsula.

Activities
By pure chance, a scandal within Thindol's royal court in Thindar in the, led to the "unmasking" of hundreds of yuan-ti agents who had secretly infiltrated the court. The reigning monarch at the time, as well as his entire family, were revealed to be yuan-ti. The entire country descended into anarchy. For months afterward, anyone suspected of possessing serpentine blood was rounded up and executed. While the executions severely weakened the yuan-ti's influence in Thindol, they also resulted in the deaths of countless innocents along the way.

The War of Unmasking ended the monarchy in Thindol and established the Council of Cities, a ruling body of government, in its stead. The Council was determined to guard Thindol's borders and prevent another surge of yuan-ti infiltrators. Sadly, they failed to realize that the yuan-ti would remain cleverly hidden beneath the waters of the Thingulph found at the heart of the country.

In addition to the overhaul of government within the country, the War of Unmasking led to Western Thindol's splitting from the rest of the country to form their own independent nation that would be called Samarach.

## Walking Out to Meet the Dragon

Walking Out to Meet the Dragon, or as it became known in the late 14 century DR - "Walking Dragon", was a friendly tavern in the town of Mosstone with its doors open to all, especially adventurers and various outcasts.

Location
Walking Out to Meet the Dragon was located on one of the back lanes of Mosstone.

Interior
Unlike the vast majority of back-alley establishments, the Dragon was a clean place with a friendly-faced and ingressive calming atmosphere. The drinking establishment offered the privacy of walled and curtained booths and the comfort of over-stuffed but mismatched seats.

The spacious taproom was centered with an enchanted fountain, the Endless Fall. Its function was not just visual, however. The bubbly, trickling sounds of the Fall were designed to drown out idle chatter and protect the guests' privacy. The fountain was an upright endless ring of flowing water waving along with a glistening pile of water-polished rocks and illuminated by candles encased in glass. The Endless Fall's enchantments drove the fountain's waters down the rocks into a glass bowl underneath and then magically drove it to "fall" up to the top of the rock stack. Candles were strategically placed at the bottom of the glass bowl and behind the trickling falling water to create a mesmerizing dance of reflected and rippling light. Even though most visitors found the show pleasant and relaxing, some claimed it made them feel seasick.

Services
Walking Out to Meet the Dragon offered moderately-priced but abundant drinks. The cellars of the Dragon held a wide variety of vintages, lacking in such rare libations as elverquisst. Travelers had a chance of finding surprising items on the tavern's drink menu.

History
The tavern was built and named by Flindarl Alaekynjack who passed about a decade before the 1370s DR. Walking Out to Meet the Dragon, the establishment's strange name was the bravest deed Flindarl had done in his life. Alone, covered in cold sweat of terror, Flindarl walked towards a rapacious pillaging dragon. The future tavern keeper acted as a distraction for a band of stalwart adventurers. As the dragon thought the single man to be an amusing distraction, the heroes slew the wyrm with an onslaught of swords and spells. Flindarl Alaekynjack emerged from the encounter crippled by the dragon's bite but miraculously alive. With his share of the dragon's hoard, Flindarl built his very own tavern.

In the years that followed, Flindarl opened his doors to numerous adventurers, creatures shunned by others, outcasts, half-orcs, disfigured folk. All were welcome at Walking Out to Meet the Dragon, earning the tavern keeper many friends. Eventually, Kardeera Alaekynjack, Flindarl's mountainous daughter, took over the tavern, just as beloved by the guests as her father was. Kardeera was a hulking and muscled woman who could hurl the tavern's tables with ease and intimidate unruly guests. Proficient swordswoman and brawler, she never hesitated to turn her own glassware in the faces of troublemakers, but just like her father, Kardeera preferred to make friends with the guests.

Rumors & Legends
One story, popularized by Volothamp Geddarm, claimed that Azoun Obarskyr IV in his youth once stopped at Walking Out to Meet the Dragon. Incognito and guarded by four War Wizards, the young prince found a long-discontinued vintage in the cellars of the tavern. He and his companions happily imbibed the entire cask of the alcohol, knowing that the king's castle long ran out of the drink.

## Wand of Prime Material pocket

A wand of Prime Material pocket was an enchanted wand capable of creating a safe, spherical pocket within any plane of existence.

Powers
The conditions within the created sphere matched those of the wielder's Prime Material plane including ground, air, and a steady temperature. The bottom one third of the sphere was ground or water, while the upper two thirds were air. While the conditions outside of the sphere were kept at bay, creatures and items could pass through the sphere's barrier.

A single expended charge created a sphere measuring  in diameter. Larger spheres could be created at an additional expenditure of one charge per additional 10 feet of diameter. Each sphere lasted between 7–13 hours unless located on the Elemental Plane of Fire, in which case they only lasted from 1–6 hours. The sphere's duration could be extended by using additional charges. A dispel magic spell would negate the sphere.

A wand of Prime Material pocket was not rechargeable.

History
Specialty priests of Waukeen called goldeyes had a chance of receiving a wand of Prime Material pocket as one of the magic items they were given upon their admission to the clergy.

## Walenburh Fengrath

Walenburh Fengrath was a mercenary of the Fengrath family and the ruling Gauntlet of the village of Shining Falls in the Delimbiyr Vale in the late 13 century DR.

History
Originally a family of mercenaries, the Fengraths were hired to escort caravans between Loudwater and  the elven colony of Thelthin'dul. However, the last caravan, some time before 1272 DR, returned and discovered all of the citizens had vanished. The few remaining elves in the caravan refused to ever come near the place, so Walenburh and the fearless Fengraths moved into the Granite Tower and declared themselves rulers of the town, later called Shining Falls. No one contested their claim.

Gauntlet Walenburh Fengrath invited strongheart halflings to settle in the village in the 1280s DR. They came and they flourished.

Relations
Walenburh was the great grandmother of Deogol Fengrath, the Gauntlet of Shining Falls circa 1372 DR.

## Wakadoshiyori

The wakadoshiyori were an office of the shogunate of Wa. They were the fourth most important office in the hierarchy of the shogunate.

Organization
The wakadoshiyori were considered subordinates of the Council of Elders, called the roju. Between two and four officials served in this role.

Like all other positions in the shogunate, to be a junior elder was a coveted role, and only the shogun could approve such an appointment.

Activities
These junior elders had similar duties to members of the roju but particularly oversaw the daimyo's hatamono, or "bannermen". They also performed a miscellany of other activities, such as inspecting public buildings or supervising the appointment of staff and counselors to the daimyo.

## Warian Datharathi

Warian Datharathi was a member of the Datharathi chaka of Vaelan in Durpar. He became the first plangent prototype in, and then left Durpar to see the world. He returned in, worried about changes in his plangent arm, and would be essential in the re-sealing of Pandorym.

History
Warian's parents died in an accident and the young man never developed a taste for the politics endemic to his family's position. He was grafted with the first plangent prototype limb in the, after losing his arm in a rockfall while inspecting a family mine. He allowed his grandfather, Shaddon Datharathi, to create a prosthetic for him; he was so thankful that he temporarily considered setting aside his plans of traveling. However, his sister Eined Datharathi recommended he see the world. He left, and did not return until the, having supported himself up to that point by gambling. His return was prompted by discovering new powers in his plangent arm, which saved his life in a tavern brawl, but at the cost of an enervating loss of vitality.

On arrival, he spent two days in town before meeting his family. In that time he came across his uncle Zeltaebar Datharathi while trying to visit his sister Eined, who was missing. In their meeting, they insisted he go see his grandfather later that evening, though they were being manipulated by Shaddon. He noticed the wrongness of the situation, and while traveling to his grandfather's workshop with Sevaera Datharathi, he found Zeltaebar had stowed away on the ship. His uncle had made similar observations as to the behavior of his siblings, Sevaera and Xaemar Datharathi.

His visit to his grandfather turned sour quickly, as the latter would not let Warian keep his present prosthetics. Only Zeltaebar's timely, stealthy intervention with a crowbar to Shaddon's head, along with Eined's arrival, provided sufficient breathing room for them to escape, though Sevaera attempted to stop them, possessed by Pandorym. They had no choice but to flee into the Celestial Nadir, where they were saved from a possessed Sevaera by Ususi Manaallin and Iahn Qoyllor. From them, he learned of Pandorym, and of Eined's death.

Warian was part of the fight with Pandorym's forces not much later that night, leading to the elder evil's final sealing on Tarsakh 19.

## Waveriding

Waveriding was a religious ceremony undergone by all waterwalkers, the specialty priests of Istishia, that celebrated their ability to summon a water elemental upon reaching a certain level of skill.

Description
This ceremony was always preceded by responsive reading and chanting that went on for an hour. Afterwards, the waterwalker would summon a water elemental and be carried by them on a riding across the waves of a large body of water. When the waterwalker finally returned from their ride, various gifts would be bestowed upon the water elemental. These included garlands of flowers, drafts of fine wine, and beautiful pieces of jewelry, pottery, or sculpture. These were said to be carried away by the water elemental back to the Sea of Timelessness, the divine realm of Istishia.

## Wagon

A wagon was a four-wheeled, open-topped vehicle hauled behind a pair of horses or other beasts of burden, used to transport goods overland. The person controlling a wagon was called a teamster.

Description
An ideal wagon was about  long and about  wide. The average model weighed about  and was designed to carry heavy loads. The usual speed of travel for a wagon was about  over smooth terrain, which, given an average amount of daylight hours, amounted to . Animals that could move faster than horses could of course make a wagon travel faster.

A wagon sold for about 35 gold pieces in most economies of Faerûn, including the harness for hitching it to the drawing animals. Teamsters typically offered their services as wagon drivers for 3 silver pieces per day.

Some wagons were equipped with a heavy or light catapult as a weapon. The craftsmen of Lantan had developed wagons with suspension systems to minimize jerky rides or special wagon wheels that could be repaired in sections. These had found popularity in mainland Faerûn.

Reputation

Merchants often came to the various trade fairs of Faerûn, selling goods from their wagons. Wagons were sometimes also used by armies to carry siege engines to battle.

In the city of Secomber, blocking a doorway or alleyway with one's wagon was a crime punishable by fine and with an edict forbidding the driving of wagon in the city again for a set time period. Parking in front of a city gate would earn a teamster a night in jail.

Types of Wagons
 Vardo, a distinct type of wagon used by the Vistani.

History
Causes
The Rensha family had ruled Loudwater and the Delimbiyr Vale since the Year of the Scourge, 1150 DR, and though they made the town prosperous and expanded its connections with the outside world, they exploited the Vale's natural resources and despoiled much of the pristine beauty of the area. Over time, the amoral Renshas descended into decadence and committed several atrocities in pursuit of their dark magic.

Into this area came a mercenary named Nanathlor Greysword, a noble of Nimbral who'd desired to establish a realm of his own in the North, and found one that needed him. In the Year of Spilled Blood, 1315 DR, Pasuuk commanded a band of talented mercenary hunters, among them Nanathlor, to bring him the horn of a unicorn, apparently for some infernal rite. But Nanathlor stayed his hand and instead defended the unicorn—in fact an avatar of the goddess Mielikki. She made him the new Green Regent and the twelve hunters who stood with him his scions, and tasked them with overthrowing the Renshas.

Battles
From then on, Nanathlor Greysword and his loyal followers were in rebellion against the rule of Pasuuk Rensha as they struggled to liberate Loudwater. They were a ragtag rebel army against Pasuuk's mercenaries and infernal powers, and would see two winters and much fierce fighting before the end. It was a little-known fact that Beyvan Keth, Red Wizard of Thay and master of the Ivy Enclave went against a treaty made with the Renshas to give support to Nanathlor, though his reasons for this remained his own.

The war culminated in the Battle of Tanglefork in the, when Nanathlor led his ragtag band against Pasuuk and his forces near Tanglefork Woods on the southern fringe of the High Forest near the River Delimbiyr. Nanathlor called on Mielikki for aid, and she awakened the trees around the battlefield and set them to attack Pasuuk's forces. In response, Pasuuk summoned a coven of devils to assault the trees. Ultimately, Mielikki's servants were victorious and the devils and Pasuuk were all killed in the fighting.

Aftermath
With the fall of the Renshas and the end of the War of the Returned Regent, Nanathlor Greysword became the new High Lord of Loudwater. Nanathlor maintained relations with other lands but ended the rampant logging and strip-mining, thus restoring the ecology and fertility of the land, and in turn renewing prosperity in the Vale.

## Warren

The Warren was a highly defensible and intricate fortress in Damara made up of hundreds of twisting tunnels beneath Bloodstone Pass. It was said to be one of the most clever and defensible sites in all of Faerûn.

Description
The Warren was a massive complex of over  of interconnected passages comprising two mazelike "nests" of tunnels, a larger one underneath the Waukeshire forest (where tunnels connected it to every home in the halfling village) and a smaller one underneath the Warrenwood. These two nests were connected via three tunnels that passed beneath the Beaumaris River.

During the mid-to-late 14 century DR, the Warren became the focal point of mutual defensive support between the halflings of Waukeshire, the dwarves of Bloodstone Mines, and the centaurs of the Warrenwood.

Geography
The Warren was located underground beneath the Valley of Bloodstone in the Galena Mountains, and stretched from beneath Waukeshire to the Warrenwood, with tunnels reaching out to Bloodstone Mine and the Damaran Gate.

Defenses
The labyrinthine tunnels were designed to be highly defensible. They were filled with clever and ingenious traps, including devastating flood pits that could be used to wipe out invaders attemptng to travel through any of the three passages that connected the nests beneath the Waukeshire and Warrenwood. All of the tunnels themselves were narrow and low, with some even feeling cramped by halfling standards, and any human or orc intruder would often need to crawl, making them easy targets. These tunnels were patrolled frequently by a halfling militia led by Sheriff Fredegast, and the entrances in both the Waukeshire and Warrenwood were watched by centaurs.

If an alarm were raised among the halflings or centaurs, both races could retreat to the Warren. The halfings of Waukeshire could disappear into hidden entrances located in their homes which connected to a single tunnel with twenty doors, each of which ostensibly led into the Warren proper, although nineteen of them were trapped with only the halflings knowing which one was safe. For the centaurs, two special chambers were created near the surface, one in the Waukeshire and one in the Warrenwood. The centaurs could retreat to these chambers, which could then be sealed shut via great iron doors, leaving the only small tunnels in or out, which allowed the halflings to bring in supplies. If forced to fight, members of the two races made formidable enemies as the halfling archers would ride into battle astride the powerful centaur cavalry.

History
The Warren was conceived during the, when the reign of Zhengyi the Witch-King over Damara and Bloodstone Pass had just begun. The dwarves of Clan Orothiar, who had been driven from Bloodstone Mines, struck a formal deal with the halflings of Waukeshire to create a mutual defense pact, with Mayor William of Waukeshire and Chief Tokan of Clan Orothiar formally declaring themselves an Alliance of Belt Watchers. Together they began digging the tunnels that would become the Warren. They worked around the clock for one month to create the initial complex beneath Waukeshire, and then added new tunnels and traps continuously over the following years.

The Warren remained a secret from the humans in order to protect it from Zhengyi's allies, with only the halflings, dwarves, and centaurs of Sleepy Wood knowing about it. The centaurs had been brought into the Alliance of Belt Watchers by the halflings, who wanted their assistance in patrolling, protecting, and providing early warnings for the Warren, which had expanded to beneath the centaurs' forest. When the Bloodstone Mines were cleared of Zhengyi's allies by Gareth Dragonsbane and his companions, the dwarves returned to their old home, but their first priority was constructing tunnels to connect their mines to the Warren. During this time, the halflings made use of these tunnels to hide their harvests in order to keep them safe from Zhengyi's tribute collectors.

The fortress remained a secret from the humans for two years until the halflings allied with Bloodstone Village and Gareth Dragonsbane during the Bloodstone Wars of the . When the army of the Duchy of Arcata invaded Bloodstone Pass, the halflings deftly flanked them from their many hidden tunnels, and when the army of the Barony of Polten set up camp in the Pass to ambush the army of Bloodstone, the halflings again ambushed them, decimating their forces and capturing every single survivor.

Following the end of the war, the halflings continued to expand the Warren, adding more miles of tunnels and more devious traps. They also began a project to connect their fortress via tunnels to the Damaran Gate, a project which was completed before the end of 1359 DR. By this time, the forest of Sleepy Wood had come to be known as the "Warrenwood" in honor of the twisting fortress beneath it.

## Wandering Pit

The Wandering Pit of Vaasa, often referred to simply as the Pit, was a mysterious entity which terrorized Vaasa in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Under normal circumstances, the Pit was completely hidden and virtually undetectable as it could perfectly camouflage into almost any terrain, whether grassy, muddy, or rocky. However, when it attacked, it opened suddenly as a  and  hole filled with spikes.

It was unclear whether the Pit was a creature, some kind of animate trap, or an amalgamation of multiple things.

Activities
The Pit roved all across Vaasa, attacking unsuspecting victims everywhere from the Sunderland and the Galena Mountains in the east to the Bottomless Bogs in the west. It would appear without warning beneath the feet of its victims, snap open suddenly and swallow them, and then just as suddenly spring shut with its prey inside. It would then vanish completely.

History
The Pit, or something similar to it, was responsible for swallowing a delegation of Red Wizards who attempted to gain an audience with Zhengyi the Witch-King in the mid–14 century DR.

The Wandering Pit gained its name and notoriety when it began attaching folk across Vaasa in the . Since it appeared shortly after the defeat of Zhengyi and the collapse of his Castle Perilous, many believed that it was some abomination or defense mechanism that had escaped from the castle. It was known to target travelers, even snatching up a rider along with their horse. In one notable case, a party of adventurers searched desperately for the Pit after they saw it snatch one of their members, but even digging  deep revealed no sign of the thing. The Pit further became a menace to the dwarves of Hillsafar Hall, who placed a 500 gold piece bounty on it.

## Warriors' Monument

Warriors' Monument, also occasionally known as the Warriors of Waterdeep, was a monument in Waterdeep's City of the Dead meant to honor all those who ever fought to defend the city.

Description
Carved of stone and standing roughly  tall, Warriors' Monument was one of the tallest structures in the City of the Dead. In addition to being an intricate statue, the monument acted as a fountain, with water gushing out from wounds on its stone figures.

It depicted a group of warriors in a circular formation, three men and two women, striking down hordes of enemies that were falling backward and outward from their circle. These enemies included barbarians, hobgoblins, orcs, trolls, and bugbears. A later addition to the monument was a warrior riding upon a griffin, high above the other stone figures, and spearing a skeletal knight with a breastplate bearing the symbol of Myrkul.

History
In 1361 DR, three years following the event known as the Time of Troubles, Warriors' Monument was renovated and magically altered to feature a minion of Myrkul. This addition was meant to commemorate those who perished fighting against the death god's invasion of Waterdeep.

## Wands Villa

Wands Villa was the family estate of the Waterdhavian noble house Wands.

Location

The Wands Villa was located in the North Ward of Waterdeep, on the corner of Magecourt and Shando Street. It stood down the road from the Hothemer Villa.

Structure
Like many Waterdhavian villas, this was a walled estate featuring a landscaped garden,Evidence of this information can be seen in how the villa is depicted on maps. with the entrance to the estate being a massive oaken gate. The building complex consisted of two high-quality buildings, one that was five stories tall and another that stood a three stories tall, one being the mansion proper and the other a guesthouse. Notably, the mansion was adorned with twin towers over a large central chamber.

On the exterior walls of the estate's buildings were hung purple banners bearing the Wands family's insignia.
Interiors
The interior of the Wands mansion featured a long marble entry hall, leading to a sweeping staircase. The second floor featured a gallery. Inside the cellars of the estate, there stood a vault of artifacts that had permanent web spells draped across their ceiling.

Inhabitants
In addition to the Wands family, the house was inhabited by iron golem servitors and a watchspider that watched over their artifact vault. If ever there was more than two intruders, the watchspider was trained to drink from a basin above the webs that held a unique variant of potion of heroism.

History
In the late 14 century DR, the Wands family put a great many magical wards in place over the villa to protect the household's members from increasing attacks by creatures from the Far Realm.

## Wand of Citrine Force

The wand of citrine force was a magical item in Ususi Manaallin's possession as of . It cast a spell similar to ethereal chamber.

Description
The wand of citrine force was a slender rod made of translucent yellow glass.

Powers
The wand of citrine force had two known modes of functioning. One of the modes of functioning cast a spell akin to ethereal chamber on the target it was pointed at, except that unlike the spell, the wand's function could function outside of the ethereal plane. Its second function was its ability to create obstacles; it could be used to block out portals, for instance, in this way. By touching the wand to its effects, those could be dispelled.The spell it most closely resembles is ethereal chamber, but its function in blocking out portals resembles no existing spell. It is possible that it duplicated a less situational version of the spell, which also had the power to create obstacles without having to target a single creature.

History
In, Ususi Manaallin held such a wand. It was almost spent; she used it five times during her quest to confront the Rotting Man, the first in order to block a portal, and the second time in order to encase the demon Eschar in a glassy case. While under the influence of the vampire Damanda, Ususi employed two charges to trap Elowen, her companion; she employed one last charge to distract the Rotting Man.

Notable Owners
Ususi Manaallin

## Watchway Tower

Watchway Tower was one of the major torchtowers of Waterdeep's Trollwall, located in the city's graveyard district, the City of the Dead.Every tower connected to the Trollwall falls under the designation of "torchtower."The sourcebook City System states that all torchtowers with names are "major towers".

Location
This tower was located at the northeastern corner of the City of the Dead. Outside of the City of the Dead, in the North Ward of Waterdeep, its exterior stood at the southern end of the walkway known as the Cliffwatch.

Structure
Watchway Tower stood four stories tall. It was directly connected to the eastern Trollwall and was situated at its northeast corner, right where it ended before the Cliffwatch as a gap, a sheer  drop that presented a grand view of the countryside east and south of the city.
Interior
Being a torchtower, Watchway Tower had all the features that were common among them. This included a debriefing room, a small armory, holding cells, storage rooms for the holding of evidence or confiscated items, some bedchambers, and some garderobes. Additionally, being a major torchtower, it had six small holding cells that could hold up to ten people, which were used in times of trouble.

Activities
Like all other torchtowers, this structure had three beacon-fires that were constantly kept on standby.

Inhabitants
Watchway Tower, like all torchtowers, was manned by members of the City Watch, who carefully watched over the edge of the Cliffwatch for any sign of invasion. Four members of the Watch were always stationed inside of the tower, while one was posted outside to watch for any trouble in the nearby alleyways abutting the City of the Dead.

## Wall Way

Wall Way was an extensive street in the Trades Ward of Waterdeep, that ran along the southern wall of the City of the Dead and part of the eastern Trollwall.

Geography
This street ran east off of an intersection of Burnt Wagon Way and Zendulth Street. It was paved in corduroy-logs until it reached the southern gate of the City of the Dead. Along the way to that gate, Wall Way acted as the northern terminus of Mhaer's Alley, Rivon Street, and Sleepers' Walk. Wall Way then continued eastward as a road paved in hard-packed dirt and gravel, being met from the south by Slipstone Street, Quill Alley, and Gaustus Street.

Wall Way then curved southward as it reached the southern Guard Tower of the City of the Dead, eventually terminating on River Street. Along the way southward to River Street, Wall Way acted as the eastern terminus of Walltower Walk and Sorn Street.

## Wafer

Wafers were thin and crisp types of usually sweet bread, eaten by itself or constructed into elaborate dessert dishes.

Usage
Apart from gastronomical use, holy wafers were known to be used as an effective weapon against vampires. Some blessed wafers were known to cure disease and cure wounds.

Availability
In Arrabar, a city-state in the Vilhon Reach, wafers were a common accompaniment of noble ladies' afternoon tea. Thin wafers were topped with whipped honey and served along with fresh expensive imported teas.
In Cormyr, Suzail's nobility, including kings, were served wafers smeared with liver pâté. A treat to some, torture to King Azoun Obarskyr IV.
Elves of Elversult made wafers using acorns. These fragile breads were served with white cheese and berry jelly.
Delectable chocolate wafers were exported out of the land of Lopango in Maztica and could be found in many places across the world, including the drow city of Menzoberranzan.

## Waterwall

Waterwall was a divine evocation spell granted to priests of Istishia. It created a wall of water that could engulf a creature or foil missile attacks.

Effects
This spell created a wall of water within a range of  from the caster. It was  tall and a minimum of  wide (wider for more advanced casters). The water appeared even if no water was available in the immediate area and lasted for at least seven minutes (longer for more experienced casters). If the spell was targeted to engulf a creature, it had a good chance to jump out of the way before the wall fully materialized.

Creatures shorter than the waterwall that attempted to pass through it were paralyzed as if by a hold person or similar effect—they were unable to continue or retreat and were helpless against melee attacks from outside. The paralyzing effect could be countered with a ring of free action or other such magics. Creatures taller than the wall were slowed to one-quarter speed. Waterwall spoiled the aim of all missiles that were fired through it. This included magic missiles and any other offensive spell that attempted to shoot through the wall. However, creatures trapped inside the watery prison could be successfully targeted.

Waterwall could be turned to ice instantly by using spells such as cone of cold or Otiluke's freezing sphere. Trapped creatures suffered the full effects of the cold-based spell.

Components
This spell required verbal and somatic components as well as a vial of water and the priest's holy symbol.

Appendix

## Wand of Ochalor's Eye

The Wand of Ochalor's Eye was an enchanted wand that functioned in a similar manner to a wand of fear.

Description
These magic wands were topped with an orange gemstone that outlasted with glow, sometimes referred to as Ochalor’s Eye, or The Eye of Fear.

Powers
Each pulse of orange light radiated a cone of immensely powerful fear spell that affected all creatures that were caught in its gaze. Affected individuals shuddered with fear, dropped anything they carried, and became paralyzed by magical terror, staring into the wand's gem. The gem's effect's remained active unless the gem was moved from the victim's eyesight or the victim was stricken or damaged. When either of these things happened, a magical trance was broken, and the affected individuals fled screaming interior. The fearsome magics of the Wand of Ochalor's Eye were surely to affect weaker creatures while the more powerful ones had a chance of resisting its mesmerizing glow.

History
Many stories surrounded the Wand of Ochalor's Eye. Some said that those who looked into the The Eye of Fear saw repeatedly replayed phantasmal images of their death. In contrast, others claimed that the gem held some unknowable alien intelligence that knew all darkest secrets mocked those affected by the wand. One thing was known for sure: those affected by Ochalor’s Eye did not remember much of the wand's magics,  yet they remembered the feelings of fear and hatred. Some survivors gained compulsory contempt of these wands, feeling an unbearable drive to destroy them on sight.

## Wall of tentacles

Wall of tentacles was a spell used by clerics of Ghaunadaur.

Effects
When cast, an elemental barrier was created. The barrier had a thickness of  and the area was determined by the caster's skill level. The inside of the barrier appeared as a wall cast in shadow, while the exterior appeared as a wall of purple-brown stone with a rough texture.

The caster and any priest of Ghaunadaur were able to freely cross this barrier. Any other creature who touched the the wall was attacked by up to four large tentacles. The wall was able to manifest up to twenty tentacles at a time, each with a maximum length of . The tentacles appeared anywhere along the wall but only four could attack a single target at a time. Usually, a creature is restrained by a single tentacle and attacked by the other three, inflicting physical damage. The tentacles could be severed.

The wall also extruded up to two large bird-like beaks. The beaks delivered a potent poison. The beaks also hissed and clacked loudly when a creature came in contact with the wall, to alert the priests of an interloper.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components, a snake and the beak from an octopus or any avian creature. The snake did not have to be alive, a dead snake was suffice.

Appendix
References

## Wayside Inn

The Wayside Inn was a hostel for travelers in the Triboar Trail region south of Neverwinter.

Location
The Wayside Inn was located at the western end of the Triboar Trail where the trail met the High Road.

Staff
In the late 15 century DR, the Wayside Inn was owned by Martisha Vinetalker. She employed Backes Dunfield as the bartender and Cooragh Struckt as the cook. The inn also contained a small smithy, which was operated by Teega.

History
Many enterprising innkeepers attempted to run an inn at the intersection of the Triboar Trail and the High Road. Some of them were successful for many years before succumbing to monsters or bandits. Others gave up soon once the dangers became obvious.

Circa 1491 DR, the Wayside Inn was attacked by undead that were created by the cult of Myrkul led by Ularan Mortus. A group of adventurers was able to defend the inn against the undead attack.

Later, the cult of Talos led by Fheralai Stormsworn occupied the inn. Unknown to the owner Martisha Vinetalker, most of her employees were members of the cult. They impaled the guests as sacrifices to the Stormbringer and tried to turn the Wayside Inn into a temple of Talos. In the process, the cultists summoned a violent thunderstorm around the inn. Ultimately, the same group of adventurers was able to stop the ritual in time. However, the Talos cultists managed to abduct Tarbin Tul, a local bard.

In the late 14 century DR, the establishment was run by a middle-aged male human innkeeper who offered libations and cheap guest accommodations with an occasional performer stemming his instrument to entertain guests.

## Wand of arcane disjunction

Wands of arcane disjunction were powerful wands that could remove magics and enchantments from their targets.

Powers
These wands were enchanted with spells that could dispel magic. Each use of the wand exhausted two charges, and at maximum, a wand could hold up to fifty charges.

History
As of the late 14 century DR, wands of arcane disjunction were a topic of heated discussion among sorcerers and wizards. Commonly, these wands were sold by enchanters to adventurers and militiamen only to be used to kill and harm fellow spellcasters. Many considered the wands in poor taste and found it disturbing that some wizards created items so deadly to their kin. Regardless of the sentiment, wands of arcane disjunction were in high demand, and merchants who sold them were only happy as heated discourse only bolstered sales.

Im the, a magical curio store – Mintassan's Mysteries of Westgate, was in possession of one of these wands.

## Warthog

Warthogs were a species of pig-like omnivores related to boars.

Description
Warthogs had large tusks that they used to defend themselves. They measured  tall at the shoulder.

Personality
Warthogs were aggressive creatures that often fought to the death, though only were aggressive when threatened or cornered or if their territory was threatened.

Ecology
Habitats
Warthogs were near exclusively found in warm climates.

On the continent of Faerûn, warthogs could be found in the Midwood and Starwood reaches of the Cormanthor forest, in the Calim Desert, in the Plain of Standing Stones, and in the Wyllowwood of Undermountain.

Beyond Faerûn they could be found on the continent of Zakhara, inhabiting badlands, forests, and hills. There they were commonly hunted by jungle giants.

Beyond the Prime Material, in the plane of Qui, a field in sector five of the plane was populated with a herd of warthogs during the Season of the Unicorn.

Members of the Watermen's Guild were responsible for supplying drinking water for Daggerford, travelling a few hundred yards upstream along the Delimbiyr River to collect fresh water. The guild also aided in suppressing fires and ensuring the city's drainage system was functioning properly.

Guildmembers always wore blue caps and used blue carts to haul water.

## Wand of misplaced objects

A wand of misplaced objects was an enchanted wand capable of magically moving a target's carried objects, usually to a decidedly inconvienient location on their person.

Powers
When activated these wands emitted golden orbs that swarmed around the target, temporarily confusing them to the point where they could not move. When the orbs vanished moments later they caused all of the target's carried items to shift to a different point on their person. The moved items could either be inconveniently relocated or not even seen at all. The impact of the sudden confusion of the item shifting caused the target to move about slowly until they rearranged their gear.

The power of a wand of misplaced objects had no effect upon items carried within a bag of holding or similar objects, however the bag of holding itself would be relocated.

These items could be recharged and each use consumed a single charge.

Notable Owners
Allet Tzuntzin, a half-elf tavern owner who lived in Waterdeep, carried a wand of misplaced objects.

## Water window

Water window was a spell used by divine spellcasters of the Malatran Plateau who did not worship gods, instead their magics were granted by the spirits.

Effects
When cast upon a body of water within, the  by  square of water began "replaying" its reflection from the previous 24 hours. The ensorcelled water source showed events and objects as long as the water's reflection captured them in the past. The spell's effectiveness depended on reflected light; if there was no light in the replayed past – the spell produced nothing but darkness. The spellcaster could issue commands to the spell's target, to show a specific object or an event. If the command was impossible, the water window turned muddy until the spellcaster issued another command to the spell's effects ended after one minute.

The spell could be cast on moving water, but when created, the water window moved along with the stream or a river.

Components
The spell's material component was the  by  square of water that became the water window.

The beam issued from a wand of salt assaulted victims with intense cramps and thirst that caused severe pain until they could drink a large amount of water. The wand could also be used to make a plot of land measuring  wide and long infertile.

These items were used by wicked followers of Tiamat.

## Warduke

Warduke was a mercenary from the world of Oerth that once served as a member of the Horned Society, and later the League of Malevolence, a band of evil adventurers that traveled together in search of greater power.

Description
While the Oerthian warrior's visage was never seen by others, he spoke with a notably raspy and sinister voice. He had beady red eyes, which were in truth fiendish grafts. The visage beneath the helm was that of a grim, hideously scarred gladiator.

Personality
Warduke took particular pleasure in hunting down his foes and reveled in the savagery with which he carried out executions. He had a particularly dark and macabre sense of humor. He liked to be surrounded by his enemies so he could fight a simple massacre. He was however not especially intelligent, and relied on others when strategy or tactics were required to complete a job.

He focused rather on his acquisition of material wealth, readily hiring himself out to anyone that could afford his services. Warduke wholly ruthless and held no mercy for himself or those he was paid to kill. In combat, he liked to hit things with his sword; it was as simple as that.

Abilities
Warduke was a brutal combatant and relentless swordsman. He could draw upon reserves of inner strength, bolstering his vitality in battle. He always fought to the death and if ever caught in the position, would never succumb to surrender. However, he would use a retreat method to break the combat off, if the enemy was an obviously superior foe, or the type that stayed out of melee range.

He used wish spells to inherently improve his physical attributes (strength, constitution, and charisma) as payment for particularly important missions.

Possessions
During his time with the Horned Society, he possessed a +3 bane vs humans anarchic flaming bastard sword made of human bone, called Nightwind. Another weapon in his hands was a +3 dagger of venom. Warduke was protected by a ceremonial spiked adamantine +3 half plate and a +5 heavy steel shield. He wore an amulet of health, gauntlets of giant strength, a pair of boots of speed, and ring of protection.

In Feywild, he wore a suit of half plate mail and his characteristic dread helm, which hid his true appearance from friends and foes alike. Warduke fought wielding a flame tongue longsword that he could ignite at will to provide illumination whenever in the dark.

Activities
When on the battlefield, Warduke gave no concern for decorum or decency. After identifying his prey, he would track down and eliminate them at an opportune moment, often at times of weakness or vulnerability.

Relationships
Warduke viewed the League of Malevolence as a means to an end, merely an accord that would be tolerated so long as it was convenient for his needs. He did regularly work with the group's leader Kelek on numerous occasions, and defended him to the death while in his employ.

History
Warduke first emerged as a figure of menace during the Greyhawk Wars on the world of Oerth.

Some unknown time later, he joined on with the sorcerer Kelek and his League of Malevolence, accompanying them on a journey to the Palace of Heart's Desire in the Feywild. It was within the palace that the League into a great battle with the rival group of adventurers known as Valor's Call. After the battle, Warduke served as Kelek's bodyguard within the fey palace, as they safeguarded an imprisoned archfey at the behest of the Hourglass Coven.

## Wayward Wardens

The Wayward Wardens were a loosely organized group of halfling paladins, clerics, and rangers that served Yondalla, the Nurturing Matriarch and leader of the halfling pantheon.

Organization
The Wayward Wardens were a fellowship of halflings that indulged their wanderlust and roamed Faerûn without settling in one place for long periods of time. Their mission was to protect and serve hin communities that were threatened by outside forces or events until such time when their services were no longer needed. They journeyed alone or in groups, patrolling roads and safeguarding settlements.

Activities
Some forty Wardens took up temporary residence with the hin of the Purple Hills during the Tethyrian Interregnum and were instrumental in preventing the violence that swept through the human community from spilling over onto their brethren.

Tactics
In a group of Wardens, those with stealthy skills would often split off and remain hidden while the rest boldly approached a situation. This allowed for surprise, rapid reinforcement, or preventing the escape of perpetrators.

## Water serpent

Water serpent was a divine conjuration spell from the elemental water sphere that animated water into the form of a large serpent.

The mass of water needed to be at least three times the caster's body volume. The water serpent followed the caster's commands to the best of its ability. In combat it could "bite" at enemies with crushing water but could not constrict them. A water serpent could not be ridden as a mount.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component two fangs from an aquatic creature to cast.

History
Water serpent was granted to priests of Umberlee, the Bitch Queen.

The wyvern-like mass of water was twice the caster's body volume. It could "bite" during combat with crushing water and could even be ridden like a mount by the caster.

Components

Water wyvern was granted to priests of Umberlee, the Bitch Queen.

## Wavetamer

Wavetamers were specialty priests devoted to Valkur, the Captain of the Waves.

Culture
Wavetamers spread Valkur's worship across the waters of Faerûn.

Abilities
A wavetamer could turn undead specific to the sea such as lacedons and sea zombies. They enjoyed a blessing from Selûne, that represented her friendly relationship with Valkur, that made it easier for them to navigate via the stars. They possessed extensive knowledge regarding sailing vessels of all shapes and sizes, making them adept at navigating treacherous waters in any craft and performing repairs.

Wavetamers possessed a number of magical abilities. Once per tenday they could conjure fruit for a crew of up to 100 sailors to ward off scurvy. They could cast the following spells once per day: weathertell, swim, control winds, ride the wind, and control weather. The following spells could be cast twice per day: trip, warp wood, straighten wood, and slow rot. They could also cast weather summoning three times per tenday and conjure air elemental or conjure water elemental once per tenday.

Possessions
In addition to blunt weapons, wavetamers commonly wielded cutlasses, harpoons, nets, and tridents. They heavist armor they wore was chainmail and they never used a shield.

## Watcher (specialty priest)

Watchers, also known derisively as Godseyes, were specialty priests devoted to Helm, He of the Unsleeping Eyes.

Activities
As with all of Helm's clergy, watchers hoped to increase Helm's followers since its decline following the Time of Troubles.

Abilities
It was difficult for others to surprise a watcher because of their constant vigilance. They could use several magical abilities on a daily basis including glyph of warding, sentry of Helm, wyvern watch, mace of Odo, exaltation, and seeking sword. They could also cast summon spectator once per tenday. A watcher's constant drilling with weaponry allowed to to strike nearly as quickly as a skilled warrior during combat.

Possessions
Watchers only wielded bludgeoning weapons. Upon their initiation into the clergy watchers were given a full suit of plate armor, a great helm, and medium shield. This armor served as their ceremonial vestment and they were required to wear it at all times, or wear no armor at all, unless an emergency prevented it. The armor was usually everbright-enchanted.
